Facebook Twitter Print Email 100 days of Ebola and the race to stop the spread 24 August 2026 Health A hundred days after the Bundibugyo Ebola outbreak was declared, the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) is facing an epidemic of an unprecedented scale and efforts must grow to contain the spread in the eastern region and across borders, UN agencies warned on Monday. Key points The World Health Organisation (WHO) reports an average of nearly 90 confirmed cases were recorded daily in during the first three months, a rate higher than the 2018-2020 outbreak in the DRC The latest figures indicate 5,515 cases and 2,642 deaths, meaning the mortality rate is almost 48 per cent The outbreak now affects six provinces, with Ituri remaining the epicentre, accounting for around 85 per cent of cases and 79 per cent of deaths WHO is calling to scale up the response in the face of ongoing situation, compounded by ongoing clashes among armed groups in eastern DRC, conflict-related displacement and growing hunger. “We can bring the Ebola outbreak under control, but only through scaled-up action in affected communities,” said WHO chief Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus. He also underlined the importance of a response led by national and local authorities, equipped with the necessary resources and supported by resolute partners. ‘Extraordinary’ progress In 100 days, the response has grown considerably in scale and capacity, with a testing network expanded from a single site to 19 laboratories now capable of analysing more than 3,000 samples daily. Treatment capacity has jumped from fewer than 10 beds to over 1,300, community engagement activities have reached more than 2.5 million people and contact tracing has climbed from nine per cent in the first week of the outbreak to 84 per cent as of 18 August. Scientific efforts have also intensified, with clinical trials of potential treatments, the evaluation of vaccine candidates and the roll-out of the first molecular diagnostic test for the Bundibugyo virus. WHO regional director for emergency response in Africa Dr. Marie Roseline Belizaire told UN News “these are extraordinary advances,” but not yet sufficient to end the epidemic or stop the transmission of the virus. 60% of deaths occur outside treatment centres Insecurity, displacement, attacks on health facilities and difficulties in accessing care continue to hamper the response. Mortality rate remains high, both in communities and amongst patients receiving care. Deaths occurring outside Ebola treatment centres accounted for around 60 per cent of the 260 weekly deaths recorded over the last six weeks. This figure highlights the ongoing difficulties in detecting cases early, referring patients swiftly to healthcare facilities and ensuring they have access to treatment, according to WHO, which stressed that interventions must be more tailored to local transmission dynamics and operational gaps, missions in active transmission hotspots must be scaled up and high-risk areas need to be well prepared before cases emerge. Tweet URL ‘People are hungry’ The Ebola epidemic is unfolding amid a wider humanitarian crisis, where communities face malnutrition and lack access to safe drinking water. “People are hungry; they need access to food; they need water,” underscored Dr Belizaire. “Humanitarian aid must also be provided alongside the Ebola response.” The challenge over the coming weeks is to stay one step ahead of the outbreak, she said, setting out five priorities: Strengthening the role of communities Stepping up surveillance and contact tracing to 95 per cent Speeding up access to care and expand capacity to 3,000 beds Improved protection for healthcare workers as 50 have died after 155 contracted the virus Strengthening cross-border preparedness Cross-border efforts in South Sudan While there are currently no Ebola cases in South Sudan, risks persist with continued population movements to and from DRC, said UN agencies. UN agencies and partners are working prevention efforts, including surveillance, screening at points of entry, training health workers, providing medical supplies and raising awareness nationwide through radio and social media. UN Humanitarian Coordinator for South Sudan Ramanathan Balakrishnan stressed the need to scale up protection for humanitarian personnel following the abduction of four aid workers by unidentified armed men in a southwestern region of South Sudan while transporting critical Ebola supplies to the border town of Libogo. Meanwhile, WHO’s regional director for Africa Dr. Mohamed Janabi issued a stark warning. “Decisions we take today will determine how quickly we can bring this outbreak under control and save lives,” he said. What’s the UN doing? WHO has deployed more than 260 experts, delivered over 330 tonnes of essential medical and logistical supplies and allocated $5.6 million to emergency operations The UN sexual and reproductive health agency, UNFPA, supports infection prevention and control measures in health facilities, engages with communities to raise awareness of preventative measures and counter misinformation and provides screenings and referrals for pregnant women and survivors of gender-based violence The World Food Programme (WFP) is providing emergency assistance and medical evacuations through its UN Humanitarian Air Service (UNHAS) Learn more about UN emergency efforts in DRC here. WHO 与非洲疾控中心对刚果民主共和国埃博拉疫苗 疫苗 的分配表示欢迎

WHO and Africa CDC welcome Ebola vaccine allocation to DRC
新闻简报世界卫生组织(WHO)和非洲疾病预防控制中心(非洲CDC)对向刚果民主共和国(DRC)分配70,000剂Ervebo埃博拉病毒疫苗疫苗以应对正在进行的Bundibugyo病毒病暴发表示欢迎。该疫苗原用于预防扎伊尔型埃博拉病毒,现正分发给一线医护人员,并将用于一项针对Bundibugyo病毒的有效性评估的3期临床试验。尽管有限的实验室和动物研究显示其可能具有保护作用,但缺乏人体证据,该试验有望为未来应对暴发提供关键数据。此次分配遵循了WHO免疫战略咨询专家组(SAGE)的建议,并属于国际疫苗应急储备(ICG)自2021年1月启动的紧急疫苗库存机制的一部分。卫生当局强调,无论是参与试验还是作为更广泛应对措施的一部分,接种疫苗者均需获得知情同意。当前重点仍是保护受影响人群和医护人员,以防止进一步的传播并挽救生命。
查看新闻五要素
时间
本周,刚果民主共和国(DRC)获得埃博拉疫苗 疫苗 的分配获批,此次 疫苗 释放遵循世界卫生组织免疫战略咨询专家组(SAGE)的建议。
地点与对象
地点为刚果民主共和国(DRC),疫苗 目标人群为一线医护人员与受影响人群。
事件
刚果民主共和国(DRC)获批接收 70,000 剂 Ervebo 埃博拉疫苗 疫苗,用于应对班迪布焦病毒病持续 暴发,此决定由国际协调组(ICG)批准。
规模、影响与风险
The allocation includes 50,000 doses for frontline health workers and 20,000 for a Phase 3 clinical trial. The vaccine is licensed for use against Zaire ebolavirus but its effectiveness against the Bundibugyo virus is not yet confirmed.
应对、状态与不确定性
此次分配属于国际协调组(ICG)自 2021 年 1 月起运行的应急 疫苗 库存机制的一部分。临床试验将评估该 疫苗 对班迪布焦病毒的有效性,卫生当局正优先保护受影响人群与医护人员。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

WHO and Africa CDC welcome Ebola vaccine allocation to DRC

News Brief
The World Health Organisation (WHO) and the Africa Centres for Disease Control and Prevention (Africa CDC) have welcomed the allocation of 70,000 doses of the Ervebo Ebola vaccine to the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) to address an ongoing outbreak of Bundibugyo virus disease. The vaccine, licensed for use against Zaire ebolavirus, is being distributed to frontline health workers and will also be used in a Phase 3 clinical trial to evaluate its effectiveness against the Bundibugyo virus. While limited laboratory and animal research suggest potential protection, human evidence is lacking, and the trial is expected to provide critical data for future outbreak responses. The allocation follows recommendations from WHO’s Strategic Advisory Group of Experts on Immunisation (SAGE) and is part of the ICG’s emergency vaccine stockpile mechanism, which has been active since January 2021. Health authorities emphasize the importance of informed consent for those receiving the vaccine, whether in the trial or as part of the broader response. The focus remains on protecting affected populations and health workers to prevent further transmission and save lives.
Original source text (English)
Facebook Linkedin Twitter Home News COP Climate Change COVID-19 Government Finance Workplace Health & Social Care Coronavirus (COVID-19) Cancer Research Diseases & Conditions Mental Health Pharma Women’s Health Environment Circular Economy Sustainable Development Agriculture Energy Research & Innovation Transport Education Technology Artificial Intelligence Digital Transformation Publications Academic Articles Special Reports Government Health & Social Care Environment Agriculture Energy Research & Innovation Transport Education Finance HR & Training Technology Stakeholders eBooks Asia Analysis North America Analysis Subscribe Search Open Access GovernmentOpen Access Government Contact Us About Us Editors Our Audience Marketing Info Pack Latest Statistics Prestige Contributors Testimonials Open Access GovernmentOpen Access Government Linkedin Twitter Facebook Instagram Home News Publications Academic Articles Special Reports Stakeholders eBooks Subscribe Agriculture Education Energy Environment COP Climate Change Circular Economy Sustainable Development Government Finance Workplace Health Cancer Research Diseases & Conditions Mental Health Pharma Women’s Health COVID-19 Research Technology Artificial Intelligence Digital Transformation Transport North America Asia Search Home Open Access News Health & Social Care WHO and Africa CDC welcome Ebola vaccine allocation to DRC Open Access News Health & Social Care WHO and Africa CDC welcome Ebola vaccine allocation to DRC August 21, 2026 image: ©gevende | iStock The Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) will receive 70,000 doses of the Ervebo Ebola vaccine as health authorities respond to an ongoing outbreak of Bundibugyo virus disease The International Coordinating Group (ICG) on Vaccine Provision approved the allocation this week after the DRC government requested access to the global Ebola vaccine stockpile. This approval is meant to support the country’s immediate response while also generating evidence about whether the vaccine can protect against the Bundibugyo virus. Vaccine allocation Of the 70,000 doses being released, 50,000 will vaccinate frontline health workers. This follows recommendations from the World Health Organisation’s Strategic Advisory Group of Experts on Immunisation (SAGE). A further 20,000 doses will be used in a Phase 3 clinical trial. The trial will assess Ervebo’s effectiveness and potential impact against the Bundibugyo virus, providing information that could help shape future outbreak responses. Ervebo is licensed and recommended for use against Ebola virus disease caused by the Zaire ebolavirus. However, it is not yet known whether the vaccine can protect people against the Bundibugyo virus. Limited evidence on protection Laboratory and animal research has provided early indications that Ervebo could offer some protection against Bundibugyo virus. However, evidence from humans is currently lacking. The clinical trial is therefore expected to play an important role in determining whether the vaccine can be safely and effectively used against the virus. The findings could provide valuable evidence for policymakers and health agencies preparing for future outbreaks. Health authorities have also stressed the importance of informed consent. People offered the vaccine, whether as part of the clinical trial or through the wider response, should understand its potential risks, benefits and limitations in relation to Bundibugyo virus. Focus on communities The World Health Organisation (WHO) and the Africa Centres for Disease Control and Prevention (Africa CDC) have welcomed the vaccine allocation and supported the DRC government’s response. Both organisations are encouraging a community-led approach, with local communities playing a central role in controlling the outbreak. Protecting affected populations and health workers remains a key priority as authorities work to prevent further transmission and save lives. Building on previous vaccine responses The ICG has managed the emergency Ebola vaccine stockpile since January 2021. The mechanism was established to help countries access vaccines quickly and equitably during major disease outbreaks. Since the Ebola vaccine mechanism was established, more than 56,000 doses of Ervebo had been allocated for Ebola outbreaks in the DRC by July 2026. Another 167,000 doses have been used in preventive vaccination campaigns for health and frontline workers in the DRC, Guinea-Bissau, Kenya, Sierra Leone and Uganda. View five news elements
Time
The allocation of the Ebola vaccine to the DRC was approved this week, with the vaccine release following recommendations from the World Health Organisation’s Strategic Advisory Group of Experts on Immunisation (SAGE).
Location and population
The Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) is the location, with the vaccine targeting frontline health workers and affected populations.
Event
The DRC received 70,000 doses of the Ervebo Ebola vaccine to respond to an ongoing outbreak of Bundibugyo virus disease, as approved by the International Coordinating Group (ICG) on Vaccine Provision.
Scale, impact and risk
The allocation includes 50,000 doses for frontline health workers and 20,000 for a Phase 3 clinical trial. The vaccine is licensed for use against Zaire ebolavirus but its effectiveness against the Bundibugyo virus is not yet confirmed.
Response, status and uncertainty
The allocation is part of the ICG’s emergency vaccine stockpile mechanism, which has been used since January 2021. The clinical trial will assess the vaccine’s effectiveness against the Bundibugyo virus, and health authorities are prioritizing protecting affected populations and health workers.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

刚果民主共和国将收到70,000剂埃博拉疫苗以应对邦迪布焦病毒暴发 - 今日土耳其

DR Congo to receive 70,000 Ebola vaccines for outbreak - Türkiye Today
新闻简报据土耳其《今日》2026年8月21日报道,刚果民主共和国(金)已收到70,000剂Ervebo 疫苗疫苗,用于应对迅速蔓延的丛林沟谷病毒暴发暴发。该暴发首次于2026年5月被检测到,已导致5,208例确诊感染和2,476例死亡,病死率为47.5%,波及6个省的56个卫生区域,成为金历史上最致命的暴发。世卫组织于2026年5月17日因缺乏获批对策将其宣布为国际关注的突发公共卫生事件。Ervebo疫苗获批用于扎伊尔埃博拉病毒,现被用于一线工作人员及开展一项评估其对丛林沟谷病毒株有效性的3期试验。早期数据显示存在一定保护作用,但疫苗的有效性仍存不确定性。包括美、英、欧盟在内的国际伙伴已承诺为应对工作提供资金支持。该暴发仍在调查中,世卫组织强调需全球团结并加强监测。
查看新闻五要素
时间
The news report was published on August 21, 2026. The outbreak was first detected in May 2026 and declared a public health emergency of international concern on May 17, 2026. The allocation of vaccines occurred in late July 2026, with the Phase 3 trial starting soon after.
地点与对象
该邦迪布焦病毒暴发发生于刚果民主共和国(DR Congo),具体涉及上韦莱省、伊图里省、北基伍省、南基伍省、下韦莱省和特舒波省等六个省份。暴发已波及56个卫生区域,影响当地大量人口,截至2026年8月初已报告5,208例确诊感染和2,476例死亡。
事件
The World Health Organization (WHO) allocated 70,000 Ervebo vaccines to DR Congo to help contain an outbreak caused by the Bundibugyo virus. The vaccines are intended for use in frontline and health workers, with 20,000 doses reserved for a Phase 3 clinical trial to assess effectiveness against the Bundibugyo strain. The outbreak was declared a public health emergency of international concern on May 17, 2026, due to its rapid spread and lack of approved countermeasures.
规模、影响与风险
该邦迪布焦病毒暴发已导致5,208例确诊感染和2,476例死亡,病死率为47.5%。暴发已波及六个省的56个卫生区域。邦迪布焦病毒此前引发的暴发病死率在30%至50%之间。世卫组织强调需全球团结并继续努力控制暴发,因尚未获批针对邦迪布焦病毒的疫苗或特定治疗方案。
应对、状态与不确定性
世卫组织及国际合作伙伴已承诺支持应对工作,包括来自美国、英国和欧盟的资金援助。疫苗分配是全球储备计划的一部分,尽管Ervebo疫苗已获批用于扎伊尔埃博拉病毒,但其对邦迪布焦病毒株的有效性仍存在不确定性。该暴发仍在调查中,世卫组织已重申其国际关注的突发公共卫生事件地位。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

DR Congo to receive 70,000 Ebola vaccines for outbreak - Türkiye Today

News Brief
The Democratic Republic of Congo (DR Congo) has received 70,000 doses of the Ervebo vaccine to combat a rapidly spreading outbreak of the Bundibugyo virus, according to a report by Türkiye Today published on August 21, 2026. The outbreak, first detected in May 2026, has caused 5,208 confirmed infections and 2,476 deaths, with a case fatality rate of 47.5%. It has affected 56 health zones across six provinces, making it the deadliest outbreak in DR Congo's history. The WHO declared it a public health emergency of international concern on May 17, 2026, due to the absence of approved countermeasures. Ervebo, licensed for Zaire ebolavirus, is being used for frontline workers and a Phase 3 trial to assess its effectiveness against the Bundibugyo strain. While early data suggests some protection, the vaccine's efficacy remains uncertain. International partners, including the U.S., U.K., and EU, have pledged financial support for response efforts. The outbreak continues to be under investigation, with the WHO emphasizing the need for global solidarity and enhanced surveillance.

LATEST COLUMNISTS NATION Politics Defense Diplomacy Diaspora Minorities REGION Europe Turkic World Middle East Balkans WORLD Africa Americas Asia & Pacific Conflicts BUSINESS Economy Finance Energy Tourism Tech Automotive LIFESTYLE Education Health Environment Travel Food Science CULTURE History Cinema Music Events Portraits Reviews SPORTS Football Basketball Volleyball Motorsports VISUALS Photo Video Infographic Newsletters USD/TRY 48.07 +0.16% world Set as preferred source NEWSLETTERS LATEST COLUMNISTS NATION Politics Defense Diplomacy Diaspora Minorities REGION Europe Turkic World Middle East Balkans WORLD Africa Americas Asia & Pacific Conflicts BUSINESS Economy Finance Energy Tourism Tech Automotive LIFESTYLE Education Health Environment Travel Food Science CULTURE History Cinema Music Events Portraits Reviews SPORTS Football Basketball Volleyball Motorsports VISUALS Photo Video Infographic Home World DR Congo to receive 70,000 Ebola vaccines for outbreak 4 A gloved laboratory worker handles a blood sample tube labeled “Ebola Virus” in this illustration image. (AA Photo) By Newsroom August 21, 2026 10:19 AM GMT+03:00 Set as preferred source Most Read 1 'Genocidaires will lose,' Erdogan says in first speech since Israel's attack in Idlib 2 Mystery surrounds US C-17 landing at Moscow's Vnukovo airport 3 How does the White House perceive the Barrack-Israel feud? 4 Türkiye's aircraft carrier set for September 2027 launch 5 11,000-year-old statue of human on leopard found at Karahantepe The Democratic Republic of Congo has been allocated 70,000 Ervebo vaccines from the global Ebola virus disease vaccine stockpile to help contain a fast-spreading outbreak caused by the Bundibugyo virus, the World Health Organization said Thursday. The government requested the release of the vaccines last week from the International Coordinating Group on Vaccine Provision. Of the total, 20,000 doses are earmarked for a Phase 3 clinical trial to assess the vaccine's effectiveness against the Bundibugyo strain. In comparison, 50,000 doses will go to frontline and health workers, following recommendations from the Strategic Advisory Group of Experts on Immunization. No vaccine or specific treatment has yet been approved for use against the Bundibugyo virus. Ervebo is licensed for outbreaks of the more commonly detected Zaire ebolavirus, and it remains unknown whether it protects humans against the Bundibugyo strain. Early laboratory and animal data suggest it may provide some protection, according to the WHO. Health workers receive travelers at an Ebola screening point at the Mpondwe Border Crossing in Uganda, May 23, 2026.(AA Photo) WHO cites global solidarity WHO Director-General Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus described the vaccine allocation as "an important example of global solidarity in action." The organization said it remains committed, alongside partners and national authorities, to strengthening surveillance, rapid detection, community engagement and vaccination efforts to bring the outbreak under control. A health worker checks a child’s temperature during Ebola screening at the Mpondwe Border Crossing in Uganda, May 23, 2026. (AA Photo) Outbreak declared global health emergency The outbreak was first detected in May, after cases of a high-mortality illness emerged in Ituri province. Laboratory analysis by the Institut National de Recherche Biomedicale in Kinshasa confirmed the Bundibugyo strain in mid-May. On May 17, the WHO declared the outbreak a public health emergency of international concern, citing its rapid spread and the absence of licensed countermeasures. It is the 17th Ebola outbreak recorded in DR Congo and, by early August, had become the fastest-growing and second-largest on record in the country. The Bundibugyo virus has caused only two previous outbreaks: one in Uganda’s Bundibugyo district in 2007-2008, from which it takes its name, and another in Isiro, DR Congo, in 2012. Case fatality rates in those outbreaks ranged from 30% to 50%, according to the WHO. A health worker checks a traveler’s temperature as Uganda steps up Ebola screening at the Mpondwe Border Crossing with the Democratic Republic of the Congo, May 23, 2026. (AA Photo) Deadliest outbreak in country's history Deaths from the current outbreak have surpassed the toll from the 2018-2020 epidemic, making it the deadliest in the country's history. Confirmed infections have reached 5,208, including 2,476 deaths, according to the latest Health Ministry update, putting the case fatality rate at 47.5%. The epidemic has affected 56 health zones across six provinces: Haut-Uele, Ituri, North Kivu, South Kivu, Bas-Uele and Tshopo. About 730 patients remain in isolation or hospital, while 1,115 people have recovered. The United States announced $112 million in bilateral assistance for personal protective equipment, screening, contact tracing and diagnostics, while the United Kingdom pledged up to £20 million ($27 million) and the European Union committed €15 million ($16.2 million) toward the response and preparedness efforts. Earlier this week, the WHO reaffirmed that the outbreak remains a public health emergency of international concern. Time
The news report was published on August 21, 2026. The outbreak was first detected in May 2026 and declared a public health emergency of international concern on May 17, 2026. The allocation of vaccines occurred in late July 2026, with the Phase 3 trial starting soon after.
Location and population
The outbreak is occurring in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DR Congo), specifically in the provinces of Haut-Uele, Ituri, North Kivu, South Kivu, Bas-Uele, and Tshopo. It has affected 56 health zones and impacted a significant portion of the local population, with 5,208 confirmed infections and 2,476 deaths reported as of early August 2026.
Event
The World Health Organization (WHO) allocated 70,000 Ervebo vaccines to DR Congo to help contain an outbreak caused by the Bundibugyo virus. The vaccines are intended for use in frontline and health workers, with 20,000 doses reserved for a Phase 3 clinical trial to assess effectiveness against the Bundibugyo strain. The outbreak was declared a public health emergency of international concern on May 17, 2026, due to its rapid spread and lack of approved countermeasures.
Scale, impact and risk
The outbreak has resulted in 5,208 confirmed infections and 2,476 deaths, with a case fatality rate of 47.5%. It has affected 56 health zones across six provinces. The Bundibugyo virus has previously caused outbreaks with fatality rates ranging from 30% to 50%. The WHO has emphasized the need for global solidarity and continued efforts to control the outbreak, as no vaccine or specific treatment has been approved for the Bundibugyo virus.
Response, status and uncertainty
The WHO and international partners have pledged support for the response, including financial assistance from the United States, United Kingdom, and European Union. The vaccine allocation is part of a global stockpile effort, and while Ervebo is licensed for Zaire ebolavirus, its effectiveness against the Bundibugyo strain remains uncertain. The outbreak is still under investigation, and the WHO has reaffirmed its status as a public health emergency of international concern.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

刚果民主共和国接收70,000剂埃博拉病毒疫苗(疫苗) 邦迪布焦型(暴发)埃博拉病毒病暴发持续扩散 - 联合国新闻

DR Congo receives 70,000 Ebola vaccine doses as Bundibugyo outbreak spreads - UN News
新闻简报2026年8月20日,刚果民主共和国(DRC)从联合国国际疫苗协调小组(ICG)收到70,000剂Ervebo 疫苗,用于应对正在扩散的Bundibugyo 暴发。该疫苗最初为针对引发2014-2016年西非埃博拉病毒病大流行的扎伊尔埃博拉病毒而研发,在DRC政府请求后获批使用。世卫组织确认了此次分配,但强调对该疫苗针对Bundibugyo毒株的有效性仍存在不确定性,并指出早期实验室和动物数据显示存在一定保护作用。ICG计划将20,000剂用于临床试验,50,000剂用于医护人员,部分居民可能在试验外接种疫苗。于2026年5月15日被宣布暴发的暴发已导致逾5,000例病例和2,300余例死亡,逾160名医护人员感染,其中40人死亡。DRC东部冲突严重阻碍了疫情防控,包括接触者追踪和安全殡葬。联合国谴责M23武装团体阻挠医疗队并造成平民伤亡。此次疫苗分配被视为应对暴发的关键举措,但该疫苗对Bundibugyo毒株的有效性仍在调查中。
查看新闻五要素
时间
刚果民主共和国接收70,000剂埃博拉病毒疫苗,邦迪布焦病毒正在扩散 2026年8月20日 健康 据报道,刚果民主共和国(DRC)将从联合国国际协调小组(ICG)处接收70,000剂Ervebo疫苗,以保护一线社区免受埃博拉病毒病侵袭。
地点与对象
刚果民主共和国(DRC),特别是受邦迪布焦埃博拉病毒病影响的东部地区。
事件
刚果民主共和国从联合国国际疫苗协调小组(ICG)获得了70,000剂针对埃博拉病毒的Ervebo疫苗疫苗,以保护一线社区免受埃博拉病毒病的侵害。该疫苗疫苗最初是为扎伊尔埃博拉病毒开发的,该病毒曾在2014-2016年引发西非埃博拉疫情。在金沙萨政府向全球埃博拉疫苗储备提出需求后,ICG批准了这批疫苗的运送。
规模、影响与风险
邦迪布焦埃博拉病毒病已导致5000余例病例和2300余人死亡。160余名医护人员感染埃博拉病毒,其中40人死亡。刚果东部冲突加剧了疫情,阻碍了医疗响应工作。世卫组织强调,Ervebo疫苗对邦迪布焦型病毒株的有效性仍不明确,但早期数据显示存在一定保护作用。
应对、状态与不确定性
联合国国际协调小组(ICG)为一项临床试验分配了20,000剂,为医护人员分配了50,000剂。受影响地区的部分居民可能在试验外接种疫苗,尽管严重副作用罕见。世卫组织对该分配表示欢迎,但指出对疫苗针对本迪布焦毒株的有效性仍存在不确定性。ICG的决定基于政府的请求与批准,但疫苗的有效性仍在调查中。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

DR Congo receives 70,000 Ebola vaccine doses as Bundibugyo outbreak spreads - UN News

News Brief
On 20 August 2026, the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) received 70,000 doses of the Ervebo vaccine from the UN’s international vaccine coordination group (ICG) to combat the spreading Bundibugyo outbreak. The vaccine, originally developed for the Zaire ebolavirus, which caused the 2014-2016 West African epidemic, was approved for use following a request from the DRC government. The WHO acknowledged the allocation but highlighted uncertainty about the vaccine’s effectiveness against the Bundibugyo strain, noting that early laboratory and animal data suggest some protection. The ICG plans to use 20,000 doses for a clinical trial and 50,000 for health workers, with some residents potentially receiving the vaccine outside the trial. The outbreak, declared on 15 May 2026, has resulted in over 5,000 cases and more than 2,300 deaths, with over 160 health workers infected and 40 fatalities. The conflict in eastern DRC has significantly hindered containment efforts, including contact tracing and safe burials. The UN has condemned the M23 armed group for its role in obstructing medical teams and causing civilian harm. The allocation of vaccines is seen as a critical step in addressing the outbreak, though the vaccine’s efficacy against the Bundibugyo strain remains under investigation.
Original source text (English)

Facebook Twitter Print Email DR Congo receives 70,000 Ebola vaccine doses as Bundibugyo outbreak spreads 20 August 2026 Health The Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) will receive 70,000 doses of the Ervebo vaccine from the UN’s international vaccine coordination group (ICG) to protect frontline communities against the Ebola virus disease. The Ervebo vaccine was initially developed to target the Zaire ebolavirus, which caused the 2014-2016 West African epidemic — the deadliest Ebola outbreak on record. The shipment is going ahead following approval from the ICG after the Government in Kinshasa requested doses from the global Ebola vaccine stockpile last week. Tweet URL Shot in the dark In a Thursday press release, the World Health Organization (WHO) welcomed the allocation but stressed that it remains unknown whether the Ervebo vaccine will work against the Bundibugyo strain of Ebola. “Early laboratory and animal data suggest it may provide some protection,” WHO wrote. “Thus, the clinical trial is expected to provide important new evidence, which is essential for policymakers to inform future use of the Ervebo vaccine.” However, given the fast-growing nature of the Ebola Bundibugyo virus outbreak, the ICG will allocate 20,000 doses for a clinical trial in the DRC and the other 50,000 doses to health workers. This means some residents of areas affected by the outbreak could receive the vaccine outside the trial, although severe side effects from the Ervebo vaccine are very rare, according to clinical trials. WHO Director-General Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus welcomed the release on Thursday, calling the move “an important example of global solidarity in action.” Cases top 5,000 Earlier this week, the Ebola outbreak, declared 15 May, became the country’s deadliest on record with 2,325 deaths. Although local authorities haven’t updated the figure since Monday, the total number of cases climbed to 5,021 according to data released on Wednesday. More than 160 health workers have contracted Ebola and 40 have died, UN Senior Ebola Coordinator Julien Harneis wrote on Thursday. The ongoing conflict in eastern DRC between the Congolese Government and the Rwanda-backed M23 armed group – along with multiple other armed groups – has hindered efforts to stop the virus’s spread by blocking medical teams from reaching conflict zones, forcing the destruction or closure of health centres and displacing thousands, making contact-tracing and safe burials nearly impossible. Rebels join Ebola fight According to news reports, the M23 armed group has touted its ability to mount an Ebola response separate from Kinshasa, including by setting up its own health administration that supervise labs and – the group claims – conduct disease surveillance. On Thursday, M23 reportedly announced that it will suspend shared taxi and boat trips to and from Ebola-affected Government-held zones to prevent the spread of Ebola in territory the group controls. The UN has sanctioned and strongly condemned the M23 militia, which has committed atrocities against civilians including forced recruitment, mass killings and sexual violence, according to “numerous reports, investigations and testimonies.” Vaccine ‘gives hope for everyone’ The ICG manages and coordinates the provision of emergency vaccine supplies and antibiotics to countries during major outbreaks. It allocated 56,000 Ervebo vaccine doses to respond to multiple Ebola outbreaks in the DRC between 2021 and July 2026. In a post on X, formerly known as Twitter, Mr. Harneis applauded the allocation announced by the ICG on Thursday. “This allocation of vaccines gives hope for everyone in the affected areas and in particular for frontline health workers,” Mr. Harneis wrote. Facebook Twitter Print Email DR Congo receives 70,000 Ebola vaccine doses as Bundibugyo outbreak spreads 20 August 2026 Health The Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) will receive 70,000 doses of the Ervebo vaccine from the UN’s international vaccine coordination group (ICG) to protect frontline communities against the Ebola virus disease.
Location and population
Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), particularly in eastern regions affected by the Bundibugyo outbreak.
Event
DR Congo received 70,000 doses of the Ervebo vaccine from the UN’s international vaccine coordination group (ICG) to protect frontline communities against the Ebola virus disease. The vaccine was initially developed for the Zaire ebolavirus, which caused the 2014-2016 West African epidemic. The ICG approved the shipment after the Government in Kinshasa requested doses from the global Ebola vaccine stockpile.
Scale, impact and risk
The Bundibugyo outbreak has resulted in over 5,000 cases and more than 2,300 deaths. Over 160 health workers have contracted Ebola, with 40 fatalities. The outbreak has been exacerbated by ongoing conflict in eastern DRC, which has hindered medical response efforts. The WHO emphasized that the effectiveness of the Ervebo vaccine against the Bundibugyo strain is still unknown, though early data suggests some protection.
Response, status and uncertainty
The UN’s ICG allocated 20,000 doses for a clinical trial and 50,000 doses for health workers. Some residents in affected areas may receive the vaccine outside the trial, though severe side effects are rare. The WHO welcomed the allocation but noted uncertainty about the vaccine’s efficacy against the Bundibugyo strain. The ICG’s decision was based on the Government’s request and approval, but the vaccine’s effectiveness remains under investigation.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

埃博拉病毒可引发重症:传播途径、症状及治疗

News article (English original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报本文讨论了埃博拉病毒病的严重性、其传播传播途径、症状及治疗。文中指出,该埃博拉病毒首次于1976年在非洲被发现,以刚果民主共和国境内的埃博拉河命名,并通过直接接触体液或污染物传播。文章还指出,潜伏期为2至21天,早期症状包括发热、肌肉疼痛和腹泻。文中提到,2014年至2016年间,西非地区发生了最大规模的埃博拉暴发,影响了几内亚、利比里亚和塞拉利昂。世卫组织报告称,自疫情始于几内亚偏远村庄以来,已造成逾11,000人死亡,实际死亡人数可能因未报告病例而更高。此外,文章提及2015年在利比里亚蒙罗维亚的一项研究中有一名埃博拉病毒病幸存者参与,并指出刚果民主共和国境内的埃博拉暴发已加剧,共报告37例病例,其中19例死亡。世卫组织强调早期识别与医学隔离的重要性以防止进一步传播。文章还提到埃塞俄比亚奥莫地区报告了3例马尔堡病毒致命病例,当地正在加强检测与预防工作。

利比里亚蒙罗维亚;西非国家;刚果民主共和国;埃塞俄比亚奥莫地区。
事件
2015年6月17日,一名埃博拉病毒病幸存者在利比里亚蒙罗维亚参与一项研究。埃塞俄比亚奥莫地区报告3例马尔堡病毒致命病例。
规模、影响与风险
自一年多前在几内亚偏远村庄暴发疫情以来,已有逾11,000人死亡。世卫组织认为实际死亡人数更高,因许多病例未被报告。刚果民主共和国境内的埃博拉暴发已加剧,共报告37例病例,其中19例死亡。早期治疗与适当支持性治疗可显著提高患者生存机会。
应对、状态与不确定性
世卫组织正参与评估暴发。检测与预防工作正在加强以避免疫情暴发。感染可能在数周内隐匿无症状,而病情急剧恶化可能使早期识别与医学隔离至关重要。

Original title (English)
Ebola Can Cause Severe Disease: How It Spreads, Symptoms and Treatment - Межа. Новини України.
News Brief
The article discusses the severe nature of Ebola virus disease, its transmission routes, symptoms, and treatment. It highlights that the Ebola virus was first identified in Africa in 1976, named after the Ebola River in the Democratic Republic of the Congo, and that it spreads through direct contact with bodily fluids or contaminated objects. The article also notes that the incubation period ranges from 2 to 21 days, with early symptoms including fever, muscle pain, and diarrhea. It mentions that the largest Ebola outbreak occurred in West Africa between 2014 and 2016, affecting Guinea, Liberia, and Sierra Leone. The WHO reports over 11,000 deaths since the epidemic began in a remote Guinean village, with the actual toll possibly higher due to unreported cases. Additionally, the article references an Ebola survivor participating in a study in Monrovia, Liberia, in 2015, and notes that the Ebola outbreak in DR Congo has intensified with 37 cases and 19 deaths. The WHO emphasizes the importance of early recognition and medical isolation to prevent further spread. The article also mentions three fatal Marburg virus cases in Ethiopia’s Omo region, with intensified testing and prevention efforts underway.

25 aug 2026, 20:03 Life360 Adds $7.99 QR Pet Tags and Expands Tracking Features Ebola Can Cause Severe Disease: How It Spreads, Symptoms and Treatment Tuesday, 25 August 2026, 10:07 An Ebola survivor takes part in a study in Monrovia, Liberia, on June 17, 2015. Liberia began a five-year effort to investigate the long-term health problems affecting survivors of the viral disease. Since the epidemic began more than a year earlier in a remote Guinean village, over 11,000 people have died, most of them in three West African countries, according to the latest World Health Organization figures. The WHO believes the actual toll is higher because many cases went unreported. Stringer/Reuters/Landov The infection can remain hidden for weeks before symptoms appear, while a rapid decline may make early recognition and medical isolation crucial. Ebola is a dangerous viral disease that can cause severe damage to the body and has a high mortality rate. The disease was first identified in Africa in 1976. It was named after the Ebola River in the Democratic Republic of the Congo, near where one of the first known outbreaks occurred. That same year, cases of the infection were also detected in Sudan, in what is now South Sudan. Based on data from CNN Types of ebolaviruses Five species of ebolavirus are known: Zaire, Bundibugyo, Sudan, Taï Forest, and Reston. Four of them can cause severe disease in humans and animals. Reston ebolavirus has been detected in animals, but no confirmed human cases caused by this species have been recorded. How the Ebola virus spreads Infection occurs through direct contact with the blood, secretions, or other bodily fluids of an infected person. The virus can also be transmitted through objects contaminated with these fluids, including clothing, medical equipment, or surfaces. Another route of infection is contact with infected animals. Ebola is not transmitted through the air and does not usually spread from a person who has not yet developed symptoms. Ebola symptoms and incubation period Symptoms most often appear 8–10 days after infection. However, the incubation period can range from 2 to 21 days. Early signs of the disease include severe weakness, fever, muscle pain, headache, diarrhea, vomiting, and abdominal pain. Some patients may also develop a rash, red eyes, chest or throat pain, difficulty breathing, or difficulty swallowing. In severe cases, internal or external bleeding may occur. Because a person’s condition can deteriorate rapidly, Ebola requires urgent medical care and isolation of the patient to prevent further spread of the virus. The largest Ebola outbreak and treatment The largest Ebola epidemic occurred in West Africa between 2014 and 2016. Guinea, Liberia, and Sierra Leone were the hardest hit. Following major outbreaks, vaccines and monoclonal antibody treatments were developed. In particular, the medicines Inmazeb and Ebanga are used to treat certain forms of disease caused by the Ebola virus. Early treatment and appropriate supportive care significantly improve a patient’s chances of survival. Ebola remains a serious public health threat in regions where outbreaks occur. Timely recognition of symptoms, contact tracing, and adherence to infection-control measures are crucial to containing the disease. CDC issues travel warnings urging caution and symptom monitoring for travelers. Learn about symptoms, transmission, treatment, and prevention. Three fatal Marburg virus cases reported in Ethiopia’s Omo region; government and international partners intensify testing and prevention efforts to avoid an epidemic. Zelenskyy Presents Golden Star to Parents of Teen Hero Stepan Chubenko Tuesday, 25 August 2026, 10:07 A family waited more than a decade for a state honor tied to a teenager whose courage became a symbol of Ukraine’s resistance. As reported by hromadske.ua. On Ukraine’s Independence Day, President Volodymyr Zelenskyy presented Stepan Chubenko’s parents with the Order of the Golden Star. The 16-year-old boy, who was killed in 2014, was posthumously awarded the title of Hero of Ukraine. The presentation of the award was announced by Vadym Filashkin, head of the Donetsk Regional Military Administration. The president personally presented the order to Stepan’s parents on August 24, 2026. Why Stepan Chubenko was awarded the title of Hero of Ukraine In October 2025, Volodymyr Zelenskyy signed a decree posthumously awarding Stepan Chubenko the title of Hero of Ukraine. Stepan was from Kramatorsk. In 2014, the boy was returning home from Kyiv via Donetsk, which was occupied at the time, as there was no direct rail connection. In Donetsk, pro-Russian militants noticed the schoolboy. He had a blue-and-yellow ribbon on his backpack, and a scarf belonging to the Karpaty football club was found among his personal belongings. Stepan was brutally tortured for several days. On July 27, 2014, the occupiers shot the 16-year-old teenager. The presentation of the Order of the Golden Star to his parents commemorated the memory of Stepan Chubenko, who was killed because of his pro-Ukrainian stance.
